<a id="hc-page-065"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.14 · PAGE 065 — 9. QUESTION-BY-QUESTION DEEP EVIDENCE ANSWERS

<a id="hc-page-066"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.15 · PAGE 066 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Caffeine?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 7 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Caffeine ingestion significantly reduces cycling completion time and enhances mean power output, with no significant effect on heart rate or perceived exertion; improvements in high-intensity interval exercise performance are observed across forms and doses, though effect sizes vary. [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1745472] [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fphys.2026.1858094]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Caffeine is typically administered 60 minutes pre-exercise in doses of 3.0–6.0 mg/kg, commonly in capsule, beverage, or dissolved forms; chronic use up to 11 weeks has been studied, but most evidence focuses on acute supplementation. [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fphys.2026.1763606]
- **Mechanism:** Caffeine enhances central nervous system drive and neuromuscular recruitment, improving performance in high-intensity efforts; it may also increase muscle energy availability and buffering capacity when combined with other supplements. [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fphys.2026.1763606]
- **Safety/interactions:** Adverse events include tachycardia, headache, insomnia, and increased energy; higher chronic consumption is linked to elevated resting heart rate and potential cardiovascular stress, particularly in adolescents or with high-dose intake. [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1766993] [B | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fcvm.2026.1718154]
- **Applicability:** Evidence is strongest for cycling and high-intensity interval exercise; generalizability to running, strength training, or long-duration endurance remains limited due to inconsistent findings and lack of long-term or diverse population studies. [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1745472] [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fphys.2026.1858094]
- **Convergence:** Both studies report significant ergogenic effects of caffeine on cycling and HIIE performance, with consistent direction across forms and doses, indicating convergence in performance outcome findings. [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1745472] [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fphys.2026.1858094]
- **Bottom line:** KEEP / REVIEW AGAINST EVIDENCE. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-067"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.16 · PAGE 067 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Creatine monohydrate?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 5 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Creatine monohydrate supplementation with resistance training improves muscle strength, endurance, and lean mass in adults; modest hypertrophy benefits observed in young adults, with no significant improvement in aerobic performance or agility. [B | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.7759/cureus.103091] [C | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.7759/cureus.103937] [C | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.3389/fphys.2024.1496544]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Dosing of 3–5 g/day is effective for maintenance; 20 g/day for 5 days may enhance performance outcomes, though not all studies show significant strength gains. [B | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.7759/cureus.103091] [C | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.7759/cureus.103937]
- **Mechanism:** Creatine increases phosphocreatine availability, improving energy supply during high-intensity exercise and reducing fatigue, with enhanced bioavailability in micronized formulations. [B | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1731306] [B | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.7759/cureus.103091]
- **Safety/interactions:** No adverse effects reported in high-dose (20 g/day) or long-term use; creatine monohydrate is GRAS and well-tolerated in healthy and athletic populations. [A | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.3389/fimmu.2026.1743603] [C | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.3389/fphys.2024.1496544]
- **Applicability:** Effective for muscle retention, strength, and hypertrophy in resistance-trained individuals; benefits may be more pronounced in younger adults and those in a caloric deficit. [B | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.7759/cureus.103091] [C | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.7759/cureus.103937] [C | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.3389/fphys.2024.1496544]
- **Convergence:** Not established in retrieved passages.
- **Bottom line:** KEEP / REVIEW AGAINST EVIDENCE. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-068"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.17 · PAGE 068 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Protein / whey?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 3 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Whey protein and leucine-enriched supplements enhance muscle protein synthesis and anabolic response in both younger and older males during resistance training, with leucine showing a stronger effect in older participants. [C | 07_supplements/eaa_bcaa | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1709867] [C | 01_food_inflammation/milk_body_composition | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1709867] [C | 07_supplements/eaa_bcaa | 10.20463/pan.2025.0026]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Combining muscle-targeted nutritional supplementation with structured exercise improves muscle strength and body composition, particularly in populations with age-related muscle decline. [B | 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands | 10.1186/s12937-025-01244-z] [B | 01_food_inflammation/milk_body_composition | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1755943]
- **Mechanism:** Leucine enrichment in protein supplements activates p70S6K1 phosphorylation and increases muscle protein synthesis, supporting anabolic adaptation to resistance training. [C | 07_supplements/eaa_bcaa | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1709867] [C | 01_food_inflammation/milk_body_composition | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1709867] [C | 07_supplements/eaa_bcaa | 10.20463/pan.2025.0026]
- **Safety/interactions:** Protein supplements may be adulterated with banned substances like anabolic steroids; however, no adverse events were reported in trials using whey protein or leucine. [A | 07_supplements/supplement_contamination_testing | 10.3389/fspor.2026.1740663] [C | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.1007/s11883-026-01426-y]
- **Applicability:** Whey protein supplementation is suitable for individuals aiming to preserve muscle mass during fat loss, especially when combined with resistance training and under a structured lifestyle intervention. [B | 01_food_inflammation/milk_body_composition | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1755943] [B | 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands | 10.1186/s12937-025-01244-z] [C | 01_food_inflammation/foods_for_body_composition | 10.3389/fnbeh.2026.1805953]
- **Convergence:** Not established in retrieved passages.
- **Bottom line:** FOOD VEHICLE / OPTIONAL. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-069"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.18 · PAGE 069 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Tirzepatide (current prescription)?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 3 unique A/B human source(s); population fit human intervention retrieved; indication/population fit requires passage-level review.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Tirzepatide at 15 mg weekly resulted in 22.5% total weight loss (23.6 kg) in adults with overweight or obesity without diabetes, compared to 2.4 kg with placebo; 10 mg dose yielded 21.4% weight loss (22.2 kg); 5 mg dose yielded 16.0% (16.1 kg). [C | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.1007/s40265-024-02029-0] [A | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.1503/cmaj.250502]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Tirzepatide was studied in 2539 adults with BMI ≥30 or ≥27 with weight-related comorbidities, without diabetes, over 72 weeks with behavioral support; doses were 5, 10, 15 mg weekly. [C | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.1007/s40265-024-02029-0] [A | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.1503/cmaj.250502]
- **Mechanism:** Tirzepatide is a dual agonist of GLP-1 and GIP receptors, reducing appetite, energy intake, and improving glucose metabolism, leading to weight loss. [A | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.7759/cureus.109350] [C | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.1007/s40265-024-02029-0]
- **Safety/interactions:** Common adverse events include mild-to-moderate nausea, diarrhea, and constipation, mostly during dose escalation; discontinuation rates were 4.3% (5 mg), 7.1% (10 mg), 6.2% (15 mg); cholecystitis occurred in 0.6% of tirzepatide users. [C | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.1007/s13300-026-01865-5] [A | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.3389/fphar.2026.1735987] [A | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.1503/cmaj.250502]
- **Applicability:** Tirzepatide is clinically appropriate for adults with overweight or obesity who struggle to maintain weight loss through behavioral intervention alone; approved for obesity and diabetes treatment. [A | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.7759/cureus.109350] [C | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.1007/s40265-024-02029-0]
- **Convergence:** Not established in retrieved passages.
- **Bottom line:** KEEP-PRESCRIPTION — PRESCRIBER MANAGED; DO NOT CHANGE DOSE. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-070"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.19 · PAGE 070 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Vitamin D3?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 6 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Vitamin D3 supplementation improves lower-limb muscle strength in athletes with baseline deficiency, but not in those with sufficient levels; no significant effect on muscle hypertrophy or strength gains beyond resistance training alone. [A | 12_population_AA | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1675654] [A | 12_population_AA | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1808314]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Supplementation with 50,000 IU vitamin D3 fortnightly (3,570 IU/day) increased serum 25(OH)D but only improved weighted reverse-grip chin-up performance in rugby players; 12-month trials show benefits in older adults with low baseline levels. [A | 12_population_AA | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1675654] [A | 12_population_AA | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1808314]
- **Mechanism:** Vitamin D enhances mitochondrial function in muscle via VDR-dependent pathways, increasing ATP-coupled respiration and protein synthesis; may reduce fall risk by improving muscle strength and function. [C | 07_supplements/vitamin_d3 | 10.1007/s00421-019-04104-x] [C | 07_supplements/vitamin_d3 | 10.3390/nu2070693]
- **Safety/interactions:** Vitamin D deficiency-related TEAEs reported in 0.99% of tirzepatide-treated participants; no severe adverse events linked to vitamin D supplementation in trials, but deficiency risk remains in high-dose users. [B | 12_population_AA | 10.1016/j.obpill.2026.100248]
- **Applicability:** General population studies show mixed results; benefits most evident in deficient individuals, especially older adults or those with low baseline levels; limited evidence in young, healthy, or non-deficient populations. [B | 12_population_AA | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1576024] [C | 07_supplements/vitamin_d3 | 10.3390/nu2070693]
- **Convergence:** Two independent RCTs indicate that vitamin D3 supplementation improves muscle strength only in individuals with baseline deficiency, supporting a targeted use case rather than universal benefit. [A | 12_population_AA | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1675654] [A | 12_population_AA | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1808314]
- **Predeclared gate:** Test 25(OH)D or establish clinician-documented deficiency; do not assume from skin tone or indoor work.
- **Bottom line:** TEST / INTAKE / CLINICIAN GATE. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-071"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.20 · PAGE 071 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Omega-3 EPA and DHA?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 2 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Omega-3 EPA and DHA supplementation shows variable effects on cardiovascular outcomes, with EPA-only formulations demonstrating stronger reductions in CVD risk in some subgroups, particularly in patients with diabetes; no significant benefit observed for cognitive improvement in established Alzheimer’s disease. [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.1007/s11883-025-01363-2] [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1581848]
- **Retrieved studies used:** For depression, a daily dose of 1.5–2.0 g EPA with 0.5 g DHA and an EPA/DHA ratio ≥2:1 is recommended; higher doses may increase gastrointestinal side effects without added benefit. [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1581848] [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1748196]
- **Mechanism:** Omega-3 PUFAs reduce triglycerides, suppress SREBP1c to inhibit fat synthesis, and exert anti-inflammatory, antithrombotic, and antiarrhythmic effects; EPA and DHA may have distinct roles in lipid and cerebrovascular health. [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.1038/s41598-025-23677-6]
- **Safety/interactions:** Daily intake up to 5,000 mg of long-chain omega-3s is considered safe; adverse effects include mild GI symptoms, and a 25% increased risk of atrial fibrillation has been reported at doses above 1 g/day. [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.3390/nu17223506] [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.1038/s41598-025-23677-6]
- **Applicability:** Benefit is greater in individuals with low baseline intake or blood levels of EPA/DHA; subgroups with low dietary PUFA intake, low baseline levels, or developmental disorders may particularly benefit. [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.1007/s11883-025-01363-2] [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1748196]
- **Convergence:** Not established in retrieved passages.
- **Bottom line:** OPTIONAL — NO CURRENT NEED. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-072"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.21 · PAGE 072 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Magnesium?

- **Coverage:** WEAK; best retrieved grade B; 1 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Magnesium supplementation in individuals with pre-diabetes and hypomagnesemia improved glycemic control, as shown by changes in fasting plasma glucose, but did not reduce systemic inflammation or affect C-peptide consistently across studies. [B | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1765308]
- **Retrieved studies used:** A 16-week RCT with 71 participants used oral magnesium supplementation to assess effects on glucose metabolism, with a target sample size of 30 per group to achieve 90% power. [B | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1765308]
- **Mechanism:** Magnesium supports mitochondrial function, reduces oxidative stress, and enhances muscle bioenergetics; Mg-malate specifically increases muscle magnesium levels and improves grip strength and rotarod performance. [C | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.1007/s13668-026-00745-5] [C | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.1007/s12011-025-04678-y]
- **Safety/interactions:** Gastrointestinal adverse events (e.g., diarrhea, nausea) were monitored in trials, particularly with magnesium oxide; serum magnesium testing requires caution in renal impairment due to poor reflection of intracellular stores. [C | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.1007/s13668-026-00745-5] [B | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1765308]
- **Applicability:** Magnesium supplementation is most relevant for individuals with confirmed deficiency or at risk due to diet, alcohol use, or chronic disease; whole-food sources are preferred, but supplementation may be indicated in deficiency states. [C | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.20945/2359-4292-2025-0374] [C | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.1007/s12011-025-04678-y]
- **Convergence:** Not established in retrieved passages.
- **Predeclared gate:** Intake/deficiency and kidney-function gate; not an automatic sleep supplement.
- **Bottom line:** TEST / INTAKE / CLINICIAN GATE. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-073"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.22 · PAGE 073 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Citrulline / citrulline malate?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 6 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Citrulline malate supplementation may improve microvascular function and muscle strength in middle-aged and older adults with type 2 diabetes, and combined L-arginine and citrulline malate supplementation significantly enhances peak power, mean power, and minimum power during high-intensity exercise compared to placebo or single-agent use. [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3390/nu16193235] [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3390/nu17172790] [C | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1788549]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Citrulline malate is commonly used in pre-workout supplements, with dosing typically ranging from 0.1 to 0.15 g/kg bodyweight, administered 60 minutes prior to exercise to optimize circulating levels. [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3390/nu16193235] [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.1038/s41598-025-16374-x]
- **Mechanism:** Citrulline enhances nitric oxide production by serving as a precursor to L-arginine, supporting vasodilation, oxygen delivery, and mitochondrial respiration, which may improve exercise performance and recovery. [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3390/nu16193235] [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.1038/s41598-025-16374-x] [A | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1632952]
- **Safety/interactions:** No adverse events were reported in studies using citrulline malate at doses up to 0.15 g/kg bodyweight, and no significant changes in liver enzymes or lactate were observed, suggesting a favorable safety profile in healthy and diabetic populations. [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3390/nu16193235] [A | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.1007/s13668-025-00672-x] [C | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1788549]
- **Applicability:** Citrulline malate may benefit individuals seeking to improve high-intensity exercise performance, reduce muscle soreness, and enhance recovery, particularly in trained or diabetic populations, though benefits in healthy, resistance-trained individuals remain inconsistent. [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3390/nu16193235] [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3390/nu17172790] [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.1038/s41598-025-16374-x]
- **Convergence:** Not established in retrieved passages.
- **Bottom line:** SHORTLIST FOR REVIEW. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-074"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.23 · PAGE 074 — What does the retrieved evidence show for HMB?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 6 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** HMB supplementation in surgical and critically ill patients shows inconsistent benefits for muscle mass and function; significant improvement in phase angle (a marker of cellular integrity) was observed, but not consistently for strength or physical performance. [A | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1621206] [A | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.1186/s12891-025-09436-8]
- **Retrieved studies used:** HMB was studied in surgical patients (n=575) and critically ill populations; most trials used 1.5–3 g/day, with durations ranging from 6 to 12 weeks, primarily in combination with protein or resistance training. [A | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1621206] [A | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.1186/s12891-025-09436-8]
- **Mechanism:** HMB reduces muscle catabolism by inhibiting the ubiquitin–proteasome pathway, stimulates protein synthesis via mTOR signaling, and may support mitochondrial function and fatty acid oxidation, contributing to improved muscle quality and metabolic efficiency. [C | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.1007/s12013-026-01995-4] [A | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.1186/s12891-025-09436-8] [B | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.1007/s10522-026-10407-2]
- **Safety/interactions:** HMB is well-tolerated at doses up to 3 g/day; no serious adverse events were reported in trials, and it is considered safe for long-term use in general populations, including older adults and those with chronic conditions. [A | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.1186/s12891-025-09436-8] [B | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.1007/s10522-026-10407-2]
- **Applicability:** HMB may be beneficial for individuals undergoing surgery or in catabolic states, but evidence is limited in healthy, active adults; its effects are not clearly separable from co-administered protein or resistance training in most studies. [A | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1505797] [A | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1621206]
- **Convergence:** Not established in retrieved passages.
- **Bottom line:** SHORTLIST FOR REVIEW. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-075"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.24 · PAGE 075 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Beta-alanine?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 5 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Beta-alanine supplementation may improve performance in repeated high-intensity efforts, such as in kickboxing and combat sports, but does not significantly affect 30-meter sprint time or perceived exertion in futsal players. [B | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.1371/journal.pone.0346898] [A | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.1186/s12902-025-02016-w]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Beta-alanine is used to enhance muscle carnosine levels, potentially supporting performance in sports requiring repeated explosive actions, though benefits in short sprints remain inconsistent. [B | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.1371/journal.pone.0346898]
- **Mechanism:** Beta-alanine increases muscle carnosine, which buffers intramuscular H+ and may improve tolerance to metabolic acidosis during high-intensity exercise. [A | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.1186/s12902-025-02016-w] [B | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.1080/15502783.2025.2564245]
- **Safety/interactions:** No adverse effects were reported in studies using beta-alanine at doses up to 6.4 g/day over 4–8 weeks; paresthesia was not observed in these trials. [B | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.1371/journal.pone.0346898] [B | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.1080/15502783.2025.2564245]
- **Applicability:** Beta-alanine may benefit athletes in combat sports or repeated explosive efforts, but evidence for sprint performance or general endurance is limited or conflicting. [B | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.1371/journal.pone.0346898] [A | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.1186/s12902-025-02016-w] [A | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1818755]
- **Convergence:** Two independent human studies (S1 and S5) suggest beta-alanine may improve performance in sport-specific, repeated high-intensity tasks, but not in short sprints, indicating partial convergence. [B | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.1371/journal.pone.0346898] [A | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1818755]
- **Bottom line:** SHORTLIST FOR REVIEW. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-076"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.25 · PAGE 076 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Ashwagandha (KSM-66 or Sensoril)?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 4 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Ashwagandha supplementation (300 mg twice daily) significantly improved muscle strength, VO2 max, time to exhaustion, and physical performance metrics in resistance-trained individuals, sprinters, and cyclists; improvements were observed in 1-RM, pull-ups, shuttle run, and aerobic capacity across multiple trials. [C | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.3389/fnut.2024.1439294] [C | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.1186/s12986-025-00902-7]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Ashwagandha (KSM-66 or root extract) was used in 8–12 week trials at 300 mg twice daily; placebo groups received starch capsules; studies included resistance-trained males, sprinters, and elite cyclists, with consistent dosing and double-blind design. [B | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.3389/frph.2025.1647721] [C | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.3389/fnut.2024.1439294]
- **Mechanism:** Ashwagandha reduces cortisol and perceived stress via HPA axis modulation; exerts anti-inflammatory and antioxidant effects (e.g., lowers MDA, boosts SOD); may promote muscle cell differentiation and improve hormonal balance, including increased testosterone. [C | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.1186/s12986-025-00902-7] [C | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.1007/s10522-025-10320-0]
- **Safety/interactions:** No significant adverse events were reported in trials lasting up to 60–90 days; hematological and serological profiles remained normal; AI-driven meta-analysis confirms safe use at standard doses, though minor lipid changes were noted. [B | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.3389/frph.2025.1647721] [B | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.7759/cureus.68940] [A | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1658265]
- **Applicability:** Ashwagandha is applicable to healthy adults engaged in resistance training, endurance sports, or sprinting; evidence supports use in males for strength, endurance, and performance; not established for females beyond one study. [B | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.3389/frph.2025.1647721] [C | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.3389/fnut.2024.1439294] [C | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.1186/s12986-025-00902-7]
- **Convergence:** Not established in retrieved passages.
- **Bottom line:** OPTIONAL — NOT SHORTLISTED. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-077"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.26 · PAGE 077 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Dietary nitrate / beetroot?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 6 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Dietary nitrate supplementation showed small positive effects on high-intensity cycling performance, particularly time-to-peak power, but no significant effects on other power output metrics in recreationally active individuals. [A | 01_food_inflammation/beets_dietary_nitrate | 10.3390/nu16162764]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Retrieved studies used beetroot juice concentrate (~300–400 mg nitrate) or raw/cooked beetroot for 2–12 weeks, primarily in adults with obesity or elevated blood pressure. [A | 01_food_inflammation/beets_dietary_nitrate | 10.3389/fpubh.2026.1798811]
- **Mechanism:** Dietary nitrate increases nitric oxide (NO) bioavailability via conversion to nitrite, reducing blood pressure and improving endothelial function, with inter-individual variability in response. [B | 01_food_inflammation/beets_dietary_nitrate | 10.1007/s00394-025-03616-x] [B | 01_food_inflammation/beets_dietary_nitrate | 10.7759/cureus.45369]
- **Safety/interactions:** No adverse events reported in studies using beetroot juice or nitrate supplements; no significant methemoglobin elevation observed. [A | 01_food_inflammation/beets_dietary_nitrate | 10.3389/fpubh.2026.1798811] [A | 01_food_inflammation/beets_dietary_nitrate | 10.7759/cureus.88590]
- **Applicability:** Evidence supports use in recreationally active individuals and adults with hypertension or obesity, but not established for muscle retention, fat loss, or running performance in cutting populations. [A | 01_food_inflammation/beets_dietary_nitrate | 10.3390/nu16162764] [A | 01_food_inflammation/beets_dietary_nitrate | 10.1093/nutrit/nuaf132]
- **Convergence:** Two independent human studies show dietary nitrate reduces systolic blood pressure, with one meta-analysis confirming a small but significant effect. [A | 01_food_inflammation/beets_dietary_nitrate | 10.3389/fpubh.2026.1798811] [B | 01_food_inflammation/beets_dietary_nitrate | 10.7759/cureus.45369]
- **Bottom line:** OPTIONAL — NOT SHORTLISTED. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.
